SM86 / RTX 3090 Ti external research consultation prompt
Generated: 2026-10-16

You are advising a GPU-systems PhD implementation team. The target is a dedicated
NVIDIA GeForce RTX 3090 Ti / GA102 / compute capability 8.6 (SM86) branch of a
Rust event-driven simulator. The complete real corpus is
../sm86_rtx_3090_capture/ (356 capture roots; 286 ok, 62 mixed-code controls,
8 retained replay/input failures; 12,392 successful launches in the current
full replay; exactly 12 simulator workers; new JSONL is .jsonl.zst). Full replay
must remain the most comprehensive default composition. NCU profiles are an
independent post-replay comparison target and must never be replay inputs,
parameter fits, or mechanism selectors. No fixture/synthetic acceptance tests,
Accel-Sim runtime/imported constants, physical-RTL claims, or authority/receipt/
hash/admission workflows are allowed.

Question
========
We have exhausted the current local literature/source inspection, real-trace
full-corpus replay, CUDA/clock64/NCU probes, bounded A/B scenarios, and observer
prototypes. Which bounded, conditional, uncertainty-aware model(s) or independent
microbenchmarks can reduce the remaining SM86 L1/L2/DRAM/cycle/IPC residuals
without violating the contracts? For each proposal give:
  * mechanism and exact causal frontier it changes;
  * required trace fields and whether it is observer-only or timing-active;
  * a locked RTX 3090 Ti probe with one-factor-at-a-time controls;
  * falsifiable predictions and failure/rollback criteria;
  * concrete Rust/Python seam and metrics to add;
  * how to evaluate full-first on all real roots while keeping raw/all-row APE
    authoritative and scope-compatible APE secondary.
Do not claim recovery of GA102 physical hashes, bank IDs, queue capacities,
engine completion, or clock crosswalks without an independent witness.

Current hard boundary
=====================
* Aggregate carded DRAM read service is active in full replay; finite
  partition/bank timing is not. H30-H37 partition/slice/pressure arrays are
  observer covariates only. The runner defaults an absolute 825 GB/s effective
  rate measured under an unlocked forced-DRAM protocol, mapped onto the simulator
  locked SM clock for cycle pricing; it is not a hardware clock crosswalk.
* Full default includes integrated L1/L2, the launch-scoped H19 store/fence
  admission candidate, source/route pressure, L1-MSHR pressure, constant-form
  observers, IPC/frontier projections, and post-replay scope diagnostics.
  Dirty writeback/coherence/visibility/payload arms are direct sim-run or
  prototype-only explicit scenarios; typed trace events gate activity. The
  corpus runner now has --no-memory-admission-feedback and suppresses the
  launch-scoped candidate for stream-l2 scope rather than emitting replay errors.
* Raw/all-row H41 mean APE is authoritative. H42 scope-compatible values are a
  secondary like-for-like view with scope-mismatch rows retained in compressed
  output but excluded from raw/all-row comparison APE. The regenerated H63
  summary.json explicitly says comparison_view=scope_compatible_secondary and
  raw_all_row_ape_included=false.

Measured residuals and unresolved questions
============================================
* L1 global-load sectors: raw 100.96% (one scope/form outlier); secondary 0.21%.
  Lookup-hit raw 30.71% / secondary 30.71%; lookup-miss raw 58.44% /
  secondary 32.39%. U16/LTC128B form semantics are unresolved; plain/U16 NCU
  controls did not show a form-specific differential.
* L2 source sectors: raw 28.24%, secondary 6.76%; lookup-hit raw 35.04%,
  secondary 11.77%; lookup-miss raw 27.00%, secondary 0%. Concentrated-route
  joins are sparse and route/pressure strata are non-monotonic.
* DRAM read sectors: raw 40.91%, secondary 26.84% on only 58 compatible exact
  joins. The trace demand interval brackets 34/58 (58.6%); dirty/coherence tails
  are present and writeback A/B is mixed. No partition/hash/service law is
  identified.
* GPC elapsed cycles 12.90%, SM-active cycles 18.02% on 107 exact joins.
  Grid/envelope and route/tail strata are non-monotonic and sparse.
* Direct PTX ld.global.LTC128B.u32 is rejected by CUDA 13.2 ptxas with
  Unknown modifier '.LTC128B'; the form exists only as a trace/cuobjdump
  observer until a supported SASS injection/generation path is found.

The attached excerpts below are the complete context available to you. Treat
observations, hypotheses, and inferences separately. Return novel, testable
ideas rather than a generic simulator survey.

# SM86 unresolved mechanism gap tracker — 2026-10-16

This ledger is intentionally not a closure checklist. It records remaining
uncertainties after H59 and the evidence needed to reduce them.

## Accuracy gaps

- **L1 raw/all-row:** global-load sectors 100.96% mean APE, dominated by a
  form/scope outlier; secondary scope-compatible view 0.21%. U16/LTC128B
  lookup semantics remain unresolved.
- **L2 raw/all-row:** source sectors 28.24%, lookup hits 35.04%, lookup misses
  27.00%. Secondary scope-compatible misses are exact (0%); hits remain 11.77%
  and concentrated-route joins are sparse.
- **DRAM raw/all-row:** read sectors 40.91%; secondary scope-compatible 26.84%
  over 58 joins. Demand intervals bracket only 58.6% of these targets.
- **Cycles/IPC:** GPC 12.90%, SM-active 18.02% on exact joins. Route/tail
  strata are confounded and high-grid envelope groups are sparse.

## Typed tails and scope gaps

- Dirty writeback and coherence tails remain unmodeled on the cycle/DRAM
  surfaces; the bounded writeback scenario is mixed and remains opt-in.
- 125 scope warnings and 67 scope-mismatch exclusions are retained in
  compressed reports. Raw/all-row residuals remain authoritative; exclusions
  are unresolved scope/model gaps, not completed mechanisms.
- Complete corpus coverage is 356 roots (286 `ok`, 62 controls, 8 failures),
  12,392 launches, 107 exact NCU identity joins; ordered fallback joins are
  metadata only.

## Required next evidence

1. Scope-matched NCU metric sets for LDG forms, L2 source sectors, and DRAM
   reads/writes.
2. Locked form-specific LDG plain/U16/LTC128B probes with reuse/warp sweeps,
   including a valid LTC128B control.
3. Locked DRAM demand probes varying one factor at a time with writeback and
   coherence completion accounted independently.
4. Larger independent route/queue probes before any partitioned timing arm.

No current gap is silently excluded, renamed, or declared physically recovered.

===== BEGIN docs/sm86_scope_aware_report_slice_20261015.md =====
# SM86 scope-aware NCU residual reporting — 2026-10-15

## Mechanism

H42 makes H40/H41 scope diagnostics operational in the post-replay reducer.
When a fixed-ratio trace-vs-NCU scope warning explicitly covers a metric, that
prediction remains in compressed machine-readable rows with status
`scope_mismatch`, but is excluded from raw/all-row comparison APE. Warning
counts and excluded rows are reported separately as a secondary like-for-like
view; no replay input, prediction selector, timing model, or NCU-derived
parameter changes.

## Evidence

H42 reduces the H41 complete full-first artifacts:

- Replay: `../sm86_replay_results/full_corpus_h41_l2_dram_scope_audit_20261015_v1/full.jsonl.zst`
- Fast: `../sm86_replay_results/full_corpus_h41_l2_dram_scope_audit_20261015_v1/fast.jsonl.zst`
- Scope-aware report: `../sm86_replay_results/metric_report_h42_scope_aware_20261015_v1/`

The retained H42 directory predates the H63 report-label correction and is
historical evidence. For machine-readable scope/raw view labels use the
regenerated H63 report:
`../sm86_replay_results/metric_report_h63_scope_label_audit_20261016_v1/`.

The corpus has 125 scope warnings and 67 explicitly excluded predictions in
the full mode. On 107 exact joins, the secondary scope-compatible mean APE
view is:

| metric | raw/all-row APE (authoritative) | secondary scope-compatible APE |
|---|---:|---:|
| L1 global-load sectors | 100.96% | 0.21% |
| L2 source sectors | 28.24% | 6.76% |
| DRAM read sectors | 40.91% | 26.84% |
| GPC elapsed cycles | 12.90% | 12.90% |

The large L1 reduction is driven by one 64× opcode-scope mismatch; L2 has
three scope warnings, while DRAM has 121. The independent NCU values remain
visible and are never used to alter simulator execution.

## Decision

Scope-aware APE is a **secondary like-for-like view**, with scope-mismatch
inventory shown separately. The all-row/raw H41 means remain the authoritative
overall residuals (L1 100.96%, L2 28.24%, DRAM 40.91%); excluded rows are
unresolved scope/model gaps, not completed mechanisms. The 26.84%
scope-compatible DRAM residual is still an active modeling target, and no
sub-10% overall closure claim is made.

===== END docs/sm86_scope_aware_report_slice_20261015.md =====


===== BEGIN docs/sm86_dram_residual_ledger_slice_20261015.md =====
# SM86 scope-compatible DRAM residual ledger — 2026-10-15

## Analysis

H43 joins H42 scope-aware DRAM rows to H37 L2 route/fill-pressure and H38 L1
MSHR pressure observers. NCU remains a post-replay target only. The retained
full corpus provides 58 scope-compatible exact DRAM joins with mean APE 26.84%.

All 58 joins are in the balanced route stratum; concentrated/incomplete route
classes have no exact DRAM joins, so route timing cannot be identified. Pressure
strata are non-monotonic and confounded:

| partition | n | mean DRAM APE |
|---|---:|---:|
| low pending-merge pressure quartile | 15 | 28.69% |
| high pending-merge pressure quartile | 14 | 12.47% |
| low new-fill pressure quartile | 15 | 40.92% |
| high new-fill pressure quartile | 16 | 8.59% |
| zero/low MSHR-stall group | 39 | 28.59% |
| high MSHR-stall group | 19 | 23.25% |

The apparent improvement at high pressure is not a transferable service law:
pressure co-varies with workload family, launch shape, and demand volume, and
the exact joins are sparse. Public partitioned-DRAM queue semantics remain a
mechanism prior only.

## Decision

No partition/hash selection, queue delay, bandwidth multiplier, or NCU-derived
correction is promoted. The active 26.84% scope-compatible DRAM residual needs
a new locked probe varying one demand factor while independently accounting for
dirty writeback/coherence tails. The existing H41 compressed replay/report are
the retained evidence base.

===== END docs/sm86_dram_residual_ledger_slice_20261015.md =====


===== BEGIN docs/sm86_dram_dirty_tail_ledger_slice_20261015.md =====
# SM86 DRAM dirty-tail residual ledger — 2026-10-15

H44 joins the 58 scope-compatible exact DRAM rows from H42 with simulator dirty
residency, dirty-eviction, and typed tail flags. All 58 rows carry both
`dirty_writeback` and `coherence` unmodeled-tail flags. No row has dirty L2
eviction sectors; the varying demand is terminal L2 dirty residency.

Dirty-residency stratification is non-monotonic:

| partition | n | mean DRAM APE |
|---|---:|---:|
| low dirty-residency quartile | 13 | 21.29% |
| middle dirty-residency quartile | 31 | 35.82% |
| high dirty-residency quartile | 14 | 12.10% |

The high-residency rows include several near-exact predictions, while other
rows at the same tail state retain large residuals. This is confounded by
read-demand volume, workload family, and launch shape; it does not identify a
writeback bandwidth, queue capacity, or coherence service law.

Decision: retain the typed dirty/coherence tails and existing optional
writeback scenario as explicit research surfaces. Do not enable default
writeback timing or infer hardware completion from terminal gauges.

===== END docs/sm86_dram_dirty_tail_ledger_slice_20261015.md =====


===== BEGIN docs/sm86_ipc_residual_ledger_slice_20261015.md =====
# SM86 IPC/cycle residual ledger — 2026-10-15

H45 joins exact H42 cycle predictions to causal frontiers, H37 L2 route/fill
pressure, H38 MSHR pressure, and typed unmodeled tails. NCU values remain
post-replay targets only.

For 107 exact joins, GPC mean APE is 12.90% and SM-active mean APE 18.02%.
Route partitions show a sparse but directionally higher residual:

| route | n | GPC APE | SM-active APE |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| balanced | 88 | 11.67% | 15.07% |
| incomplete | 15 | 16.08% | 31.76% |
| concentrated | 4 | 28.03% | 31.32% |

All but four exact rows carry dirty-writeback/coherence tails. Issue-terminal
frontier gap and pending-merge pressure partitions are non-monotonic; high
pressure can coincide with either lower or higher residuals. The concentrated
class is too sparse for a service/timing law, and incomplete routes mix tail and
launch-shape effects.

Decision: no IPC/cycle timing correction is promoted. Preserve separate issue,
SM-active, GPC, and tail frontiers; require a larger independent route/queue
probe before any conditional timing arm.

===== END docs/sm86_ipc_residual_ledger_slice_20261015.md =====

# SM86 DRAM demand interval audit — 2026-10-15

H46 audits the existing trace-derived DRAM demand interval (L2-missing
sectors, compulsory instruction sectors, constant-domain candidates, and source
envelopes) against H42 scope-compatible independent NCU DRAM read rows.

Of 58 scope-compatible exact joins, the interval brackets 34 targets (58.6%).
The interval's median low/high bounds are 262/392 sectors, while the target
median is 392. High residuals frequently lie above the interval, but demand,
workload family, and NCU operation scope remain confounded; the largest outlier
is adjacent to a previously flagged scope mismatch.

No multiplier, bound expansion, or timing change is promoted. The interval is
retained as a falsifiable demand observer; a new source-domain capture must
separate DRAM read demand from writeback/coherence and profiler operation scope
before expanding it.

===== END docs/sm86_dram_demand_interval_slice_20261015.md =====


===== BEGIN docs/sm86_writeback_scenario_negative_20261015.md =====
# SM86 dirty-writeback scenario A/B — negative promotion — 2026-10-15

H47 exercised the existing bounded `CardedWriteback` path on five real
high-terminal-dirty traces. The capacity was predeclared at 4,096 sectors; it
was not selected from NCU values. Default full replay was compared with the
explicit writeback scenario at the same 825 GB/s service rate.

| workload | default GPC APE | writeback GPC APE | effect |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| qkv-rope standard | 29.19% | 24.92% | improves |
| RMSNorm forward | 12.34% | 11.51% | slight improve |
| Hyena FFT | 8.38% | 7.26% | slight improve |
| Flash attention noncausal | 23.72% | 23.72% | unchanged |
| fused linear/GELU/GRN | 24.69% | 26.16% | worsens |

SM-active frontiers are unchanged because writeback is a post-issue tail. The
mixed outcome does not identify a transferable capacity or service law, and
the existing probes do not observe hardware completion ownership. Writeback
timing remains an explicit opt-in scenario; default full replay is unchanged.

===== END docs/sm86_writeback_scenario_negative_20261015.md =====


===== BEGIN docs/sm86_dram_sota_queue_prior_slice_20261016.md =====
# SM86 DRAM SOTA queue prior — 2026-10-16

H57 reviewed retained GPGPU-Sim/Accel-Sim source and local SOTA notes. Generic
modern simulators model per-partition private/shared credits, finite
L2→DRAM/DRAM→L2/L2→interconnect queues, and round-robin subpartition arbitration
(`gpgpu-sim/l2cache.cc`, `l2cache.h`, `dram_sched.cc`). Huerta's Ampere model
adds finite memory-subcore/PRT/coalescer structures.

These mechanisms are source-inspection priors only; no Accel-Sim binary/runtime
or imported constant is used. They motivate the existing SM86 route/pressure
observers, but do
not identify GA102's physical partition hash, queue capacities, row policy, or
completion ownership. H43 pressure residuals are confounded and H47
writeback A/B is mixed; no generic queue law transfers to 3090 Ti.

Decision: retain aggregate carded DRAM timing plus observer-only modulo/XOR
pressure and typed writeback/coherence tails. Do not import generic queue
capacities or activate partitioned timing without a locked SM86 probe isolating
one demand factor.

===== END docs/sm86_dram_sota_queue_prior_slice_20261016.md =====

# SM86 constant-form L1 lookup residual ledger — 2026-10-16

H51 joins H48 form counters to exact independent NCU L1 lookup rows without
excluding observations or fitting parameters. Form groups are selected from
trace-side static counters only.

| form | hit joins | hit mean APE | miss joins | miss mean APE |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| other/plain | 32 | 5.85% | 43 | 31.53% |
| U16 | 42 | 51.36% | 50 | 34.90% |
| LTC128B | 0 positive-target | — | 3 | 26.68% |
| no constant form | 3 | 6.89% | 10 | 25.25% |

The LTC128B hit domain has zero positive NCU targets, so no hit APE is defined;
its global traffic remains modeled. U16 is materially different from
plain/other lookup behavior, but the residual is still broad and the form
probe found no stable timing differential.

Decision: retain source-qualified form observers, preserve global traffic, and
defer any U16/LTC lookup correction until a scope-matched reuse/working-set
probe establishes the client semantics. No NCU values enter replay or fitting.

===== BEGIN docs/sm86_constant_form_ncu_probe_slice_20261016.md =====
# SM86 constant-form independent NCU probe — 2026-10-16

H54 profiled the rebuilt notes probe with NCU independently (no replay or
parameter fitting). Plain 32-bit `__ldg` and uint16 `__ldg` used identical work
and produced identical operation domains at two points:

| point | global-load inst/requests | sectors | lookup hits/misses |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| 1 warp, 64 iterations, reuse 1 | 64/64 | 256 | 252/4 |
| 8 warps, 64 iterations, reuse 256 | 512/512 | 2,048 | 1,764/284 |

Both variants retain distinct SASS (`LDG.E.CONSTANT` vs
`LDG.E.U16.CONSTANT`) but no independent lookup-domain differential appears in
these two controls. This is mechanism evidence only: it does not prove all
forms/general workloads equivalent and is not consumed by the simulator.

Decision: retain H48 form observers and defer form-specific lookup/timing
correction pending a broader controlled sweep including LTC128B.

===== END docs/sm86_constant_form_ncu_probe_slice_20261016.md =====


===== BEGIN docs/sm86_constant_form_sweep_slice_20261016.md =====
# SM86 constant-form reuse/warp sweep — 2026-10-16

H50 extends the notes probe with 24 real RTX 3090 Ti points: plain 32-bit
`__ldg` versus uint16 `__ldg`, 1/4/8 resident warps, and reuse distances of
1/4/32/256 lines. Each pair uses identical addresses, iterations, and
functional work. All points pass checksums; SASS remains
`LDG.E.CONSTANT` versus `LDG.E.U16.CONSTANT`.

Clock64 ranges increase with reuse distance and pressure, as expected, but
plain/U16 differences are small and non-monotonic (for example, W1/reuse1
maxima 9,257 vs 9,258 cycles; W4/reuse32 23,089 vs 21,574; W8/reuse256
39,123 vs 39,108). These are mechanism observations, not a stable form-latency
law.

Decision: retain H48 form counters and defer lookup/timing correction. A future
NCU campaign may use this matrix as independent form-specific scope evidence,
but no NCU target is consumed by replay or parameter selection.

===== END docs/sm86_constant_form_sweep_slice_20261016.md =====


===== BEGIN docs/sm86_ltc128b_probe_blocker_slice_20261016.md =====
# SM86 LTC128B probe boundary — 2026-10-16

H61 attempted a direct PTX form-specific probe in the notes project using:

```ptx
ld.global.LTC128B.u32
```

CUDA 13.2 `ptxas` rejects the modifier deterministically:
`Unknown modifier '.LTC128B'`. The form is a SASS-level encoding not exposed by
the supported PTX frontend. The existing H48 corpus traces and cuobjdump
observations remain the valid LTC128B evidence; forcing an inline opcode or
binary patch would invalidate functional provenance and is not attempted.

Decision: retain LTC128B as an observer-only form, defer an independent
LTC128B NCU sweep until a supported SASS-generation/injection path is available,
and do not infer timing/cache semantics from the toolchain failure.

===== END docs/sm86_ltc128b_probe_blocker_slice_20261016.md =====


===== BEGIN docs/sm86_plan_consistency_audit_slice_20261016.md =====
# SM86 plan/source consistency audit — H63 — 2026-10-16

## Adversarial findings and fixes

The H30–H62 source/docs review found two concrete semantic defects:

1. H42 prose and the metric reducer described scope-compatible rows as
   “primary” even though scope-mismatch rows are excluded from the raw/all-row
   comparison. The reducer/report now says that rows remain compressed and are
   excluded from raw/all-row APE; its compared table is explicitly a secondary
   scope-compatible view. STATUS and the H42 compact report use the same labels.
2. The plan's DRAM section described a finite partition/bank controller as if it
   were already the active full implementation. The source currently uses
   `DramBw::Carded` aggregate read service (default effective-rate scenario),
   H30–H37 partition/route pressure as observer covariates, and the H19
   store/fence feedback candidate. The plan now marks finite partition/bank
   timing as a future evidence-gated arm and keeps dirty writeback,
coherence/visibility, and payload forwarding explicitly optional/typed
unavailable by default; typed trace events gate activity and flags configure
those optional arms.

The H57–H62 plan/status/memory entries were also reordered chronologically, and
H62 now names aggregate carded DRAM plus observer-only partition candidates.
No observer is promoted to a physical hash, queue capacity, row/bank identity,
completion timestamp, or proprietary topology claim. Additional source
consistency fixes rename modeled cache state/trace line-domain observers that
were called “physical,” document source-only GPGPU-Sim/Accel-Sim inspection,
and label the 825 GB/s value as an unlocked absolute-rate hypothesis mapped
onto the locked simulation clock.

The corpus runner now exposes an explicit `--no-memory-admission-feedback`
ablation, defaults the launch-scoped H19 candidate only for `cache_scope=launch`,
and records `memory_admission_feedback_scope_suppressed=true` for stream-L2
scope. This prevents a direct-run launch-scope validation error from turning a
stream-L2 replay into all-failure rows. Per-launch DRAM counters now retain the
configured service rate.

## Post-replay evidence

The corrected reducer was run against the retained real H41 full/fast artifacts
(no simulator replay and no NCU value used as an input):

- Output: `../sm86_replay_results/metric_report_h63_scope_label_audit_20261016_v1/`
- Input full/fast artifacts remain compressed `.jsonl.zst`.
- Full scope warnings: 125; explicit scope-mismatch exclusions: 67.
- Fast scope warnings/exclusions: 0/0.
- The full table is headed “scope-compatible compared metrics”; mismatch rows
  remain in compressed `metric_comparisons.jsonl.zst`.
- Raw/all-row H41 values remain the authoritative dashboard baseline; no APE
  value was converted into a completion or sub-10% claim.
- Machine-readable summary fields identify `comparison_view=scope_compatible_secondary`,
  `raw_all_row_ape_included=false`, and the scope-mismatch policy.
- A real stream-L2 full replay of `simbench_l1_vendor_sgemm_cublas` completed
  `ok` with the compressed artifact
  `../sm86_replay_results/h63_stream_l2_scope_20261016_v1/full.jsonl.zst`;
  the summary records candidate suppression and the launch row records the
  configured 825,000,000,000 B/s service rate.

## Checks

- `python3 tools/sm120_metric_report.py ...` completed on the real H41 corpus
  artifact and emitted compressed comparison/summary files.
- `python3 -m py_compile tools/sm120_corpus_replay.py tools/sm120_metric_report.py`
  passed.
- Release `sim-trace`/`sim-run` builds and
  `cargo check --manifest-path sim/Cargo.toml --workspace --all-features`
  passed.
- `git diff --check` passed.

Remaining raw/secondary residuals and typed tails are unchanged and tracked in
`docs/sm86_unresolved_gap_tracker_20261016.md`; this slice corrects the contract
language and report semantics without claiming new model accuracy.

===== END docs/sm86_plan_consistency_audit_slice_20261016.md =====

===== BEGIN plans/3090ti_full_replay_modeling_plan.md: outcome/contracts =====
    1: # RTX 3090 Ti / SM86 full-replay modeling plan
    2: 
    3: **Status:** design baseline after repository, corpus, and prior-research audit
    4: **Date:** 2026-10-16
    5: **Branch:** `3090ti_full_replay`
    6: **Target:** GeForce RTX 3090 Ti / GA102 / compute capability 8.6 / `sm_86`
    7: **Corpus:** `../sm86_rtx_3090_capture/`
    8: 
    9: This is the active implementation plan for this branch. The branch is
   10: 3090-Ti-only: SM120/RTX-5090 compatibility, merge choreography, and preserving
   11: 5090-specific CLI or model assumptions are not design constraints. Existing
   12: SM120-oriented seams may be refactored in focused SM86 work, while unrelated
   13: behavior remains preserved until its replacement path is proven. The target is
   14: an effective, conditional, uncertainty-aware model rather than a claim to
   15: recover proprietary RTL.
   16: 
   17: ## 1. Outcome and completion bar
   18: 
   19: ### 1.1 Requested outcome
   20: 
   21: Build a default **full replay** path that executes every admitted real SM86
   22: trace through one coherent model:
   23: 
   24: ```text
   25: enhanced NVBit trace + SASS/control sidecars
   26:   -> typed dynamic transaction stream
   27:   -> per-SMSP issue/dependence/occupancy model
   28:   -> sectored L1TEX
   29:   -> six-slice effective L2
   30:   -> finite interconnect/partition queues
   31:   -> banked effective GDDR6X/DRAM service
   32:   -> fills, acknowledgements, writeback, visibility, and warp readiness
   33:   -> separate issue/event/launch/tail frontiers and IPC projections
   34: ```
   35: 
   36: The full mode is the default and composes all **supported and evidence-backed**
   37: capabilities at once; it must not silently fall back to flat timing when a
   38: cache, queue, or IPC component is selected. The landed SM86 path currently
   39: uses the integrated L1/L2 hierarchy, the aggregate carded read-service arm
   40: with the independently measured effective-rate scenario, the bounded
   41: store/fence admission candidate, and observer-only partition/route pressure.
   42: This is an effective conditional service model, not a physical row/bank claim.
   43: Finite per-partition/bank timing, resident dirty writeback, coherence,
   44: payload forwarding, and physical row/bank mapping remain future or explicit
   45: scenario arms until their independent evidence and typed inputs exist. Full
   46: mode still emits typed demand/lower-bound/tail fields for those gaps and never
   47: invents a timing constant or silently falls back to flat timing.
   48: 
   49: ### 1.2 Practical completion bar
   50: 
   51: The plan is complete when all of the following are true:
   52: 
   53: 1. The runner discovers the complete real corpus (currently 356 capture roots,
   54:    of which 354 are contract-clean and two have typed-invalid trace-contract
   55:    dispositions; 15,165 discovered kernel launches, 107,533,357 instructions
   56:    and 118,262 blocks), and emits a per-root/per-launch coverage matrix recording
   57:    dynamic launches reported, launches selected/replayed, records replayed,
   58:    omissions, and typed input dispositions. “Complete corpus” means every
   59:    discovered launch has a row or explicit disposition; it does not assume that
   60:    every trace record is replayable.
   61: 2. Every `status=ok` full replay row contains instruction, L1, L2,
   62:    partition/queue, DRAM demand/service, completion-tail, and IPC/frontier
   63:    fields, or a typed unavailable/open-tail reason scoped to the
   64:    mechanism/configuration. `input_error`, `timeout`, and `replay_error` rows
   65:    carry a failure taxonomy and never fabricate metrics.
   66: 3. L1/L2 traffic and hit/lookup projections are produced for all launches whose
   67:    trace carries the required address/control information; unsupported address
   68:    classes remain separate rather than being counted as hits.
   69: 4. DRAM read demand, writeback demand, read/write service, and service-delay
   70:    projections are produced independently. Completed DRAM writes are not
   71:    inferred from a terminal dirty gauge.
   72: 5. IPC projections use SASS-level instruction identity, compiler control bits,
   73:    scoreboard readiness, resource/operand pressure, memory response events,
   74:    and occupancy. PTX instruction counts are never substituted for SASS issue
   75:    work.
   76: 6. NCU values are joined only after replay. Existing profiles are treated as
   77:    one selected launch per root unless an exact launch identity exists; the
   78:    plan does not claim 15,165-launch NCU accuracy from 356 single-launch
   79:    profiles.
   80: 7. Results are reported by workload family and mechanism residual. A single
   81:    scalar score is not used to select topology, queue, or service parameters.
   82: 8. The complete-corpus run uses exactly twelve simulator workers and writes new
   83:    generated JSONL as `.jsonl.zst`, with isolated per-root logs/results.
   84: 
   85: ## 2. Research baseline and current state
===== END plans/3090ti_full_replay_modeling_plan.md: outcome/contracts =====


===== BEGIN plans/3090ti_full_replay_modeling_plan.md: model sections =====
  340: 
  341: ## 4. Target full-replay model
  342: 
  343: ### 4.1 Typed transaction and address stream
  344: 
  345: Introduce a generic SM86 transaction adapter (in `sim/mem`, shared with
  346: `sim/sm`) around the existing `AccessKind`, `MemRequest`, and sector-expansion
  347: records. Migrate incrementally rather than creating a parallel object graph:
  348: 
  349: ```text
  350: Transaction {
  351:   launch/cta/warp/lane/pc/occurrence identity,
  352:   issue_cycle and producer frontier,
  353:   address_space and cache operator,
  354:   operation class: load/store/atomic/reduction/async/texture/surface/local,
  355:   memory_scope/order, return-value-use, alias class,
  356:   virtual line, allocation_id/generation/status, sector mask, byte mask,
  357:   logical data width and transfer width, predicate/active mask,
  358:   source/destination route, requester SMSP/SM,
  359:   parent/child request identity and generation,
  360:   completion requirements: data_ready, ack, visible, group/barrier,
  361: }
  362: ```
  363: 
  364: Formation must preserve per-lane widths and addresses until sector expansion is
  365: complete. Coalesce only at a declared hierarchy boundary; retain both the
  366: logical instruction count and lower-level sector/request count. This is needed
  367: for the 69-sector misalignment anchor, local-wide accesses, RED RMW traffic,
  368: and operator-specific L1 routing.
  369: 
  370: Local addresses require thread-local stack/frame canonicalization before any
  371: line comparison; shared addresses require bank/cluster scope; allocation reuse
  372: and cross-allocation records invalidate physical-owner assumptions. Missing
  373: `cache_op`, `memory_op_access_kind`, lane addresses, allocation identity or
  374: transfer width is typed unknown, not a legacy default. Texture/surface remains
  375: unavailable until a distinct tracer/classifier route exists.
  376: 
  377: ### 4.2 SM86 issue, scheduler, and IPC model
  378: 
  379: Keep `IssueModelParams::from_card` and `IssueResourceSource::Carded` as the
  380: carded SM86 source of truth. Add a thin `Sm86FullConfig` wrapper/extension for
  381: new queue, operand, frontend and IPC fields instead of wholesale-renaming the
  382: existing issue configuration:
  383: 
  384: - four SMSPs per SM, one issue transaction per SMSP per cycle by default, no
  385:   dual issue unless an explicitly measured probe changes the card;
  386: - compiler-declared stall/yield/read-barrier/write-barrier fields from SASS;
  387: - per-warp scoreboard and register readiness, variable-latency producers, and
  388:   memory response tokens;
  389: - CTA residency from traced block geometry, registers, shared memory, 48-warp
  390:   and 16-block ceilings, with explicit placement/co-residency assumptions;
  391: - SASS-level pipeline classes (FP/INT, SFU, LSU, tensor HMMA/IMMA/TF32,
  392:   control/barrier) with measured latency and initiation intervals;
  393: - a configurable warp-selection policy (initially current deterministic policy,
  394:   then oldest/youngest/greedy-eligible candidates) scored against independent
  395:   issue/throughput probes, never selected from a workload's NCU result;
  396: - optional register-bank/reuse-bit pressure and instruction-stream buffer
  397:   envelopes. The mechanism is enabled only when static operands/control data
  398:   are present; otherwise it emits a typed unavailable contribution.
  399: 
  400: Compute IPC from several denominators:
  401: 
  402: ```text
  403: ipc_issue_window       = issued_instructions / issue_frontier_cycles
  404: ipc_sm_active          = issued_instructions / SM-active frontier
  405: ipc_sm_elapsed         = issued_instructions / launch/envelope frontier
  406: ipc_smsP_active        = per-SMSP issued / per-SMSP active frontier
  407: ipc_memory_aware       = issued_instructions / max(issue, memory, writeback, visibility)
  408: ```
  409: 
  410: Retain separate instruction numerators: issue attempts, successfully executed
  411: instructions, replayed instructions, and records retired. NCU
  412: `smsp__inst_issued.sum`, `smsp__inst_executed.sum`, and their SM rollups are
  413: not interchangeable; if the trace cannot identify replay/redo causes, the
  414: corresponding IPC comparison is unavailable rather than silently using issue
  415: attempts as executed work.
  416: 
  417: The report must retain raw frontiers and the causal readiness histogram so a
  418: low IPC can be attributed to long scoreboard, short scoreboard, LSU throttle,
  419: tensor/resource throttle, barrier, front-end, occupancy, DRAM service, or tail.
  420: 
  421: Do not revive the rejected composite LDGSTS front-end constants from the August
  422: 2026 experiment. The measured experiment worsened transfer MAE and interaction
  423: signs. If LDGSTS issue-cliff behavior is revisited, run the declared direct
  424: 40-cell barrier-free assay first and keep it an independent candidate.
  425: 
  426: ### 4.3 L1TEX model
  427: 
  428: Replace the current SM120/effective-L1 framing with `Sm86L1Config`:
  429: 
  430: - 32-B sectors and 128-B lines;
  431: - cache-operator route: `.ca`/eligible `LDG.E`/local eligible traffic to L1,
  432:   `.cg` to the measured L2-only route, and `.cs`, atomics, texture/surface and
  433:   LDGSTS kept as explicit probe-dependent routes rather than assumed to share
  434:   one bypass path;
  435: - configurable effective capacity per shared-memory carveout, using the
  436:   counter-free p-chase intervals (base approximately 98,304 B with an upper
  437:   transition bracket near 163,840 B; carveout-specific values remain separate);
  438: - configurable sets/ways/index hash/replacement (current four-way/256-set
  439:   card as the starting declaration, p-chase associativity/replacement as
  440:   unresolved intervals);
  441: - line allocation and resident-sector fills, sector-valid masks, MSHR merge,
  442:   finite fill/request/response queues, hit-under-miss, retry, and eviction;
  443: - L1 lookup/sector counters split by Global/Local/Store/Atomic/Reduction and
  444:   operator route; write-through/no-allocate remains the explicit baseline until
  445:   dirty resident/writeback probes justify the resident-hit policy;
  446: - shared-memory wavefront/bank accounting remains outside L1 traffic but feeds
  447:   the shared pipeline and IPC readiness.
  448: 
  449: Texture/surface records are not currently represented as distinct `AccessKind`
  450: variants. They remain typed unavailable until tracer/classifier and hierarchy
  451: scopes are admitted; they must never be silently folded into Global traffic.
  452: 
  453: The current `L1Cache` state machine is a good semantic base. The required
  454: change is to separate state semantics from geometry policy and to make service
  455: queues explicit instead of letting latency alone stand in for admission.
  456: 
  457: ### 4.4 Six-slice L2 model
  458: 
  459: Refactor `L2Model` into a GA102 configuration with:
  460: 
  461: - 6,291,456 B capacity, six slices, 128-B lines, 32-B sectors;
  462: - configurable slice hash and set mapping (the existing six hash candidates
  463:   become measured/effective candidates, not an arbitrary `LineMod` default);
  464: - set-associative replacement candidates (LRU, SRRIP, BIP/PLRU where observed);
  465: - non-inclusive state, write-allocate stores, partial-sector validity, dirty
  466:   masks, generation-safe pending fills, and per-source lookup accounting;
  467: - per-slice request queues, MSHR/pending-request merge, response queues,
  468:   cross-SM arbitration, and bounded retry/backpressure;
  469: - optional stream/persistent L2 scope only when capture device/stream identity
  470:   proves a single ordered stream; launch-cold remains the comparison lane;
  471: - L2 prefetch/operator hints represented as explicit transactions, not as an
  472:   unexplained hit-rate multiplier.
  473: 
  474: The old L2 predictor's historical CV and same-cell correction factors are
  475: starting diagnostics only. Future geometry selection must use independent
  476: address/capacity/hash probes and family-disjoint replay residuals.
  477: 
  478: ### 4.5 Fabric, partition, and DRAM service
  479: 
  480: Add an effective lower-memory service layer between L2 and DRAM:
  481: 
  482: 1. **L2-to-fabric packetization:** map each fetched/writeback sector to a
  483:    request packet with source SM/slice, operation, priority, and byte count.
  484: 2. **Partition mapping:** implement a parameterized address-to-partition/bank
  485:    mapping. Start with a small predeclared candidate set and infer bounds from
  486:    stride/partition-throughput probes; do not copy constants from another SKU.
  487: 3. **Finite queues (planned timing arm):** request, return, writeback, atomic,
  488:    and maintenance queues must eventually have explicit capacities/occupancy,
  489:    with admission and service as separate frontiers. H30–H37 currently expose
  490:    route/slice/fill/pressure observers only; they do not impose a hidden GA102
  491:    queue capacity or timing delay.
  492: 4. **DRAM controller (current versus planned):** current full replay uses the
  493:    aggregate fixed-point carded read server and its declared effective-rate
  494:    interval. A future timing-active arm may model partition/queue service with
  495:    bounded FR-FCFS-like/open/closed-row candidates, burst service, and
  496:    read/write high-watermark arbitration only after an independent locked-clock
  497:    probe and typed mapping witness. Never derive physical row IDs from virtual
  498:    address bits. Use the 1,008 GB/s card peak only as an upper/service
  499:    hypothesis; infer effective efficiency from independent streaming and mixed
  500:    read/write probes. Physical effective service-class IDs remain unavailable
  501:    unless an explicit effective mapping arm is declared; physical
  502:    partition/bank/row IDs remain unavailable without a physical-page/controller
  503:    witness.
  504: 5. **Overlap:** issue independent reads to different banks/partitions; serialize
  505:    only where the configured resource or inferred conflict requires it. Preserve
  506:    fixed-point arithmetic and never charge the same latency twice.
  507: 6. **Writes:** distinguish store acknowledgement, L2 dirty eviction, terminal
  508:    dirty flush, DRAM write service, and profiler-visible write counters.
  509: 7. **Atomics/reductions:** account for RMW fetch sectors, line/partition
  510:    serialization and returned-value readiness independently from ordinary loads.
  511: 
  512: The existing `DramBw::Carded` aggregate server is the **current** full-mode
  513: read-service implementation, with H30–H37 partition/route counters retained as
  514: observer covariates. The finite partition/bank controller is a future
  515: timing-active implementation slice; until it has independent evidence and a
  516: real-trace differential, it must not be described as active or substituted by
  517: an imported topology constant. `DramBw::CardedWriteback` remains an explicit
  518: scenario arm rather than a default completed writeback model.
  519: 
  520: ### 4.6 Visibility, coherence, and asynchronous operations
  521: 
  522: Wire existing opt-in primitives into the SM86 full path with explicit scopes:
  523: 
  524: - L1 visibility observations and invalidation queue for cross-SM global writes;
  525: - L2 directory-like effective ownership (read/read sharing, write/atomic
  526:   invalidation/owner transfer) with sector granularity;
  527: - `MEMBAR`, CCTL invalidation, store acknowledgement, and fence readiness as
  528:   distinct event classes;
  529: - `cp.async`/LDGSTS source visibility, group commit/wait, destination shared
  530:   wavefronts and transport as separate dependencies;
  531: - constant/immediate cache and shared-bank paths as independent resources;
  532: - payload/value ledgers only when the sidecar supplies a complete generation and
  533:   identity witness; otherwise preserve a typed unknown tail.
  534: - virtual-address aliases, page translation/TLB/page-walk effects, cache
  535:   compression/ECC effects, and physical-page identity remain explicit unknowns
  536:   unless a sidecar witness exists; alias atomics cannot be treated as ordinary
  537:   coalesced requests.
  538: - Multiple CUDA streams, ordered CUDA events, concurrent kernels, copy-engine
  539:   work, context switches and host launch gaps are separate dependencies. When
  540:   the trace lacks a total cross-stream order, do not serialize them by
  541:   simulator sequence; mark launch-envelope/cache carry-in as partial-order
  542:   unknown and report the omitted overlap domain.
  543: 
  544: These mechanisms are model-local effective semantics. Full replay enables every
  545: implemented, evidence-backed observer/model by default, including the current
  546: aggregate read service and store/fence feedback candidate. Bounded dirty
  547: writeback, acknowledgement extensions, and visibility/coherence arms activate
  548: only when typed trace events make the mechanism applicable and their explicit
  549: scenario configuration is supplied; the default runner does not synthesize
  550: those optional flags. Otherwise it retains typed
  551: unavailable tails and interval uncertainty rather than silently inventing
  552: completion. Payload forwarding and physical ownership remain unavailable
  553: without witnesses. None of these arms claims proprietary ownership, router,
  554: or engine state.
  555: 
  556: ### 4.7 Frontiers and reports
  557: 
  558: Every launch report must include:
  559: 
  560: - exact instruction/record/sector/request counts;
  561: - issue attempts, executed instructions, replayed instructions and retired
  562:   records with explicit semantic domains;
  563: - L1 lookup/hit/miss/merge/stall counters and route splits;
  564: - L2 lookup/hit/miss/merge/eviction counters by source and effective slice;
  565: - DRAM read demand, atomic/reduction RMW demand, dirty/writeback demand,
  566:   effective partition/service-class demand, queue delay, and completion counts;
  567:   row/bank hits and physical partition IDs are
  568:   `Unavailable(physical_mapping_absent)` unless their explicit effective arm is
  569:   active;
  570: - issue, modeled event, SM active, launch envelope, visibility, writeback,
  571:   terminal tail, and typed unknown frontiers;
  572: - IPC projections with numerator/denominator/endpoint metadata;
  573: - readiness-cause cycles and critical-path contributors;
  574: - configuration identity and uncertainty intervals.
  575: 
  576: For every L1/L2/fabric/partition/DRAM queue, expose enqueue, dequeue-to-service,
  577: service-complete, in-flight, queued occupancy (excluding in-flight), and maximum
  578: occupancy. The invariant is
  579: `enqueued - dequeued_to_service - in_flight = queued_occupancy` and
  580: `dequeued_to_service - service_complete = in_flight` after accounting for
  581: cancelled/stale generations. Reject negative/overflow states, duplicate parent
  582: completion, unbounded retry, and deadlock/nontermination.
  583: When finite queues are disabled, the aggregate G/D/1 differential arm must
  584: match the latency-only/reference result within declared rounding semantics.
  585: 
  586: If a metric is absent in both retained replay modes, the NCU report must place
  587: it in one compact family-grouped “currently unavailable” section with exact
  588: names/counts in machine-readable output. Do not print repeated `N/A` tables or
  589: raw NCU distributions for unavailable metrics.
  590: 
  591: ### 4.8 Required composition ablations
  592: 
  593: The all-capabilities full mode is already the default for the implemented
  594: evidence-backed components. Run the same real roots through this declared
  595: differential ladder whenever a **future timing-active** finite-service,
  596: writeback, atomic, or visibility arm is proposed for composition:
  597: 
  598: ```text
  599: flat frontier
  600:   -> L1 only
  601:   -> L1 + L2 state
  602:   -> L1 + L2 + aggregate read service
  603:   -> + partition/queue service
  604:   -> + writeback/atomic service
  605:   -> + visibility/coherence arm
===== END plans/3090ti_full_replay_modeling_plan.md: model sections =====


===== BEGIN plans/3090ti_full_replay_modeling_plan.md: implementation/probes =====
  654: - Retain per-kernel `sm80_code_on_sm86_runtime` metadata for the current
  655:   version-80 inventory and preserve `code_arch=80` alongside `runtime_arch=86`.
  656:   Do not silently relabel it to native SM86; mixed roots need per-function
  657:   disposition rather than root-wide rejection.
  658: - Emit the exact parser contract expected by `ControlSidecar`: normalized
  659:   function names without `___digits`, `0x`-prefixed high/low words, control
  660:   layout shift 41, decoded stall/yield/read/write/wait fields, and summary
  661:   counts. Reconcile the producer's wire `yield_bit`/`control_fields.is_yield`
  662:   semantics explicitly; do not copy an inverted-looking field blindly.
  663: - Keep dynamic contract identifiers as producer provenance; route behavior from
  664:   decoded memory space/opcode/architecture, not a string containing `sm120`.
  665: 
  666: ### 5.3 Memory crates
  667: 
  668: - `sim/mem/src/access.rs`: add the transaction adapter/operation metadata and
  669:   share sector/byte-mask formation across L1/L2/DRAM without duplicating the
  670:   existing `AccessKind`/`MemRequest` state machine in one large rewrite.
  671: - `sim/mem/src/l1.rs`: split geometry/policy/service from tag state; add set/way
  672:   indexing, finite request/response queues and route-specific counters.
  673: - `sim/mem/src/l2.rs`: make six-slice GA102 geometry and effective hash/policy
  674:   configurable; add slice queue and partition handoff metadata.
  675: - `sim/mem/src/writeback.rs`, `coherence.rs`, `l1_visibility.rs`: wire the
  676:   existing bounded primitives into full mode with typed event ownership,
  677:   generation checks, and separate completion frontiers.
  678: - Add `sim/mem/src/partition.rs` and `sim/mem/src/dram_controller.rs` for
  679:   effective partition/bank/row service. Keep fixed-point arithmetic and checked
  680:   overflow; expose a simple aggregate ablation for differential diagnosis.
  681: - `sim/semantics/src/dram.rs` and related boundary-state types must serialize
  682:   the new queue/bank state without making a digest/receipt an execution gate.
  683: 
  684: ### 5.4 SM issue and system event engine
  685: 
  686: - `sim/sm/src/issue/config.rs`: extend the carded SM86 issue path with a thin
  687:   full-mode wrapper, per-pipeline intervals, scheduler policy,
  688:   register/operand pressure, and frontend model.
  689: - `sim/sm/src/issue.rs`: connect transaction issue to cache admission and
  690:   queue backpressure; preserve compiler barriers and per-warp readiness.
  691: - `sim/sm/src/system.rs`: retain `DramBw::Carded` as the current full-mode
  692:   aggregate read-service arm. A future evidence-backed slice may replace it
  693:   with the effective partition/DRAM controller only after differential replay;
  694:   until then H30–H37 partition candidates remain observer-only.
  695: - `sim/sm/src/replay.rs`: use one shared L1/L2/system instance per launch or
  696:   explicit stream scope. Preserve simulator event ordering while marking
  697:   hardware cross-SM order partial when the trace lacks a witness.
  698: - Add partition/service model state and new `Ev` variants in
  699:   `sim/sm/src/system.rs` first, preserving current event phase order and typed
  700:   snapshots. Defer a generic `sim/engine` phase API until real-trace
  701:   differential replay proves it does not perturb existing event boundaries.
  702: 
  703: ### 5.5 Direct runner, metrics, and tools
  704: 
  705: - `sim/direct_run/src/main.rs`: replace SM120 flags with an SM86 full-mode
  706:   default; keep a flat/fast mode only as a complementary frontier.
  707: - `sim/direct_run/src/metrics.rs` and `emit.rs`: emit all SM86 families,
  708:   typed intervals and separate frontiers; preserve exact unavailable reasons.
  709: - Refactor `tools/sm120_corpus_replay.py` into the architecture-parameterized
  710:   complete-corpus runner required by the repository contract; an optional
  711:   `tools/sm86_corpus_replay.py` may be a thin entrypoint. Preserve exactly
  712:   twelve workers, per-root logs, timeout/output bounds, cleaned tracer
  713:   environment, compressed `.jsonl.zst`, and full-before-fast ordering.
  714: - Add `tools/sm86_corpus_compare.py`/`sm86_metric_report.py` to join exact
  715:   selected NCU launches and report unmatched/full-corpus coverage separately.
  716: - Add post-replay reports for family residuals, L1/L2 sensitivity, DRAM service,
  717:   IPC causes, and frontier decomposition. These are descriptive reports, not
  718:   replay gates.
  719: 
  720: ## 6. Measurement and probe program
  721: 
  722: All probes below are real CUDA/NVBit/NCU captures or retained real sidecars.
  723: They are used to constrain independent model parameters and to expose
  724: mechanism-local residuals; scored corpus NCU rows are not fed back into the
  725: same launch's configuration.
  726: 
  727: ### M0 — host/corpus and trace contract
  728: 
  729: - First ingest/qualify retained real sidecars and probe reports already under
  730:   `/home/jakeshea/GPU_sims_notes` (L1 admission/arrival, L2 queue frontier,
  731:   memory-retirement/store-ack matrices, scheduler contention, DRAM streams,
  732:   atomics, cp.async, prefetch/persist and carveout studies). Record each
  733:   artifact's independent/development status and rerun only missing mechanism
  734:   gaps; do not treat historical snapshot labels as authority.
  735: - Independently query GPU name, UUID, CC, device index, driver, CUDA, clocks,
  736:   memory clock, and P-state on the 3090 Ti host.
  737: - Reconcile the 356 roots (354 clean + 2 typed-invalid), nested trace count,
  738:   327 catalog rows, 356 profiles, the version-80 inventory, stale metadata
  739:   labels, and diagnostics.
  740: - Generalize static sidecar generation and replay the three verticals plus one
  741:   native SimBench L1/L2/DRAM root in flat and full modes.
  742: - Differentially compare the new metadata converter against the three retained
  743:   real control sidecars (FP32, HMMA, cp.async), comparing PCs/function IDs/
  744:   control/opcode fields; any mismatch fails closed for that root.
  745: - Completion evidence: every row records explicit architecture disposition,
  746:   sidecar source, selected launch identity, dynamic-reported versus
  747:   records-replayed counts, and unsupported/unmapped address counts; no
  748:   generated plain JSONL.
  749: 
  750: ### M1 — L1 route, capacity, and admission
  751: 
  752: Use qualified retained real SimBench/capture roots first, then fresh independent
  753: probes only for gaps, for:
  754: 
  755: - `.ca`, `.cg`, `.cs`, `.cv`, bare `LDG.E`, local, atom, RED, texture/surface;
  756: - aligned 32/64/128-bit, misaligned, partial warp and variable-width accesses;
  757: - working-set/capacity sweeps under carveouts 0/25/50/75/100%;
  758: - set-conflict stride grids and replacement/re-reference patterns;
  759: - dependent, ILP4, ILP16 and many-warp miss streams for MSHR/admission;
  760: - L1 request/response queue and fill-under-miss saturation.
  761: 
  762: Record L1 sector lookups, hit/miss, line allocations, sector fills, merges,
  763: MSHR stalls, queue delay and NCU lookup/sector comparison fields. Produce an
  764: effective interval card only from predeclared counter-free/public/independent
  765: probe evidence; NCU values remain post-replay diagnostics and cannot select or
  766: fit the card.
  767: 
  768: ### M2 — L2 slices, hash, replacement, and persistence
  769: 
  770: - Ingest qualified retained L2 queue/prefetch/persist/thrash sidecars before
  771:   scheduling new GPU runs; preserve falsifier and unknown-order dispositions.
  772: - Sweep footprints around 1/2/3/4/6/8/12/16 MiB with dependent and parallel
  773:   streams to distinguish L2 hit latency from capacity.
  774: - Use stride and base-offset families to infer slice/partition mapping and
  775:   conflict sets; keep candidate hashes as a partial order when not identifiable.
  776: - Contrast LRU/SRRIP/BIP/PLRU candidates with re-reference distance and
  777:   thrashing streams.
  778: - Exercise `.cg`, atomics, RED, stores, prefetch hints, persisting windows,
  779:   local memory and multi-SM sharing.
  780: - Measure launch-cold versus same-stream persistence only where launch identity
  781:   proves a valid scope.
  782: 
  783: Output slice/hash likelihood bands, effective set/way candidates, request
  784: coalescing/PRT behavior, L2 queue bounds, and family-specific residuals.
  785: 
  786: ### M3 — partition/fabric/DRAM and writeback
  787: 
  788: - Ingest retained memory-retirement, store-ack, DRAM-stream and atomic service
  789:   matrices first; keep instruction-mismatch and lower-bound-falsifier cells out
  790:   of parameter selection while retaining their residuals.
  791: - Streaming read, write, read/write mix, random, row-hit/row-conflict and
  792:   bank-conflict probes at multiple strides and working sets.
  793: - Sweep active SM/CTA/wrap counts and independent request depth to expose finite
  794:   partition and DRAM queue throughput.
  795: - Exercise 4-MiB and 16-MiB dirty stores, L2 evictions, terminal dirty
  796:   residency, atomics and RED/RMW.
  797: - Measure effective bandwidth under the locked operating point and no-clock
  798:   control first; later clock sweeps are separate calibration evidence.
  799: - Record read/write arbitration, service delay, row hit/miss, queue occupancy,
  800:   writeback completion, DRAM sector/byte counts and profiler-visible tails.
  801: 
  802: The output is an effective DRAM service interval and queue/bank candidate set,
  803: not a claim of exact GDDR6X controller topology. Without an independently
  804: supported mapping/service arm, row-hit/miss and achieved-bandwidth fields stay
  805: typed unavailable while demand and lower-bound service remain reportable.
  806: 
  807: ### M4 — IPC and scheduler mechanisms
  808: 
  809: - Ingest retained scheduler/issue-port/frontend/constant-load reports first;
  810:   quarantine known falsifier cells (including issue-port, SFU, constant and
  811:   result-queue mismatches) rather than clipping their residuals.
  812: - SASS-level dependent/independent FP32, INT, SFU, HMMA FP16/BF16, TF32,
  813:   IMMA INT8, sparse HMMA, ldmatrix/stmatrix, shared-bank,
  814:   branch/reconvergence, barrier and mixed-memory roots.
  815: - Vary warp count, CTA count, register footprint, shared-memory carveout,
  816:   compiler stall/yield/dependency fields, operand reuse bits and source/dest
  817:   register-bank patterns.
  818: - Separate issue, active, memory-ready, barrier, front-end and terminal tails.
  819: - Add a dedicated control-flow arm for SSY/BRA/SYNC/reconvergence stacks,
  820:   predicated-off issue, divergent path occupancy and fetch-loop effects; do not
  821:   hide these under ordinary scoreboard or barrier cycles.
  822: - Measure SASS instruction throughput/latency, scheduler policy, register-file
  823:   pressure, instruction-stream footprint and memory-pipeline contention.
  824: 
  825: Select bounds only from predeclared independent probe observations, public
  826: facts, or counter-free clock measurements. NCU counters remain post-replay
  827: comparison targets even for probe workloads. Predeclare family partitions
  828: (native SimBench/SOL, adapted controls, substitutes, snapshots) and leave one
  829: partition out when reporting generalization; use full-corpus residuals only for
  830: post-hoc diagnosis, not iterative parameter selection.
  831: 
  832: ### M5 — full-corpus replay and post-replay comparison

  865:

  867:

  875:    L2, aggregate effective DRAM read service, observer-only partition/route
  876:    candidates, atomic/RMW accounting, IPC projections, and the defaulted
  877:    evidence-backed store/fence feedback candidate. The SM86 runner explicitly
  878:    enables `--carded-dram` and records the default 825 GB/s effective-rate
  879:    hypothesis; it also auto-selects the launch-scoped store/fence candidate.
  880:    Resident dirty writeback, coherence/visibility, and payload forwarding
  881:    remain typed unavailable in the complete-corpus runner: their numeric/flag
  882:    scenarios are currently direct `sim-run`/prototype-only and are not silently
  883:    synthesized by the corpus driver. Do not relabel observer-only partition
  884:    candidates as physical service. Persistent `stream-l2` scope suppresses the

  168: def replay(capture: Path, relative: str, control: Path, args, mode: str, logs: Path,
  169:            scope_override: str | None = None) -> dict:
  170:     memory_mode = args.memory_mode if mode == "full" else args.fast_memory_mode
  171:     cache_scope = scope_override or args.cache_scope
  172:     command = [str(args.sim_bin), "--trace", str(capture), "--control", str(control),
  173:                "--arch-card", str(args.arch_card)]
  174:     if args.target_arch == "sm_86" and args.pchase_card and memory_mode != "flat":
  175:         command.extend(("--pchase-card", str(args.pchase_card)))
  176:     command.extend(("--mode", str(memory_mode)))
  177:     if args.target_arch == "sm_120":
  178:         command.append("--sm120-alpha")
  179:     if args.sm120_effective_l1 and args.target_arch == "sm_120" and memory_mode != "flat":
  180:         command.extend(("--sm120-effective-l1", args.sm120_effective_l1))
  181:     if args.sm120_effective_l2 and args.target_arch == "sm_120" and memory_mode == "l1-l2":
  182:         command.extend(("--sm120-effective-l2", args.sm120_effective_l2))
  183:     if args.carded_dram_enabled and mode == "full":
  184:         command.append("--carded-dram")
  185:         if args.dram_service_rate_bytes_per_sec is not None:
  186:             command.extend(("--dram-service-rate-bytes-per-sec", str(args.dram_service_rate_bytes_per_sec)))
  187:     if mode == "full":
  188:         command.extend(("--cache-scope", cache_scope))
  189:         command.append("--ncu-shaped-metrics")
  190:         if args.memory_admission_feedback:
  191:             command.extend(("--memory-admission-feedback", str(args.memory_admission_feedback)))
  192:     if mode == "fast":
  193:         command.append("--fast-frontier-only")
  194:     suffix = ".fallback-launch" if scope_override else ""
  195:     stderr_path = logs / (relative + suffix + ".stderr")
  196:     started = time.perf_counter_ns()
  197:     environment, removed_environment = _clean_replay_environment()
  198:     row: dict = {}
  199:     stdout_path: Path | None = None
  200:     try:

  293:     parser.add_argument("--carded-dram", action=argparse.BooleanOptionalAction, default=None,

  295:     parser.add_argument("--dram-service-rate-bytes-per-sec", type=int, default=None,
  296:                         help="explicit effective DRAM service-rate scenario; SM86 full defaults to the independently measured 825 GB/s hypothesis and records the override")
  297:     parser.add_argument("--memory-admission-feedback", type=Path, default=None,
  298:                         help="override the default launch-scoped SM86 store/fence admission+completion candidate; stream-l2 suppresses this arm; static gate remains enforced in sim-sm")
  299:     parser.add_argument("--no-memory-admission-feedback", action="store_true",
  300:                         help="explicit SM86 ablation: do not auto-select the launch-scoped store/fence candidate")
  301:     # Accepted for old command lines; full replay always emits these projections.

  303:                         help=argparse.SUPPRESS)

  305:     parser.add_argument("--max-sim-output-bytes", type=int, default=512 * 1024 * 1024,
  306:                         help="bounded simulator stdout retained by the driver")
  307:     parser.add_argument("--workers", type=int, default=12,
  308:                         help="concurrent simulator processes (must be exactly 12)")
  309:     args = parser.parse_args(argv)
  310:     args.capture_root = args.capture_root.resolve()
  311:     args.output = args.output.resolve()
  312:     args.sim_bin = args.sim_bin.resolve()
  313:     args.arch_card = args.arch_card.resolve()
  314:     args.pchase_card = args.pchase_card.resolve() if args.pchase_card else None
  315:     try:
  316:         card_arch = card_architecture(args.arch_card)
  317:     except ValueError as error:
  318:         parser.error(str(error))
  319:     if args.target_arch != "auto" and args.target_arch != card_arch:
  320:         parser.error(f"--target-arch {args.target_arch} conflicts with architecture card {card_arch}")
  321:     args.target_arch = card_arch
  322:     args.memory_admission_feedback_defaulted = False
  323:     args.memory_admission_feedback_scope_suppressed = False
  324:     args.memory_admission_feedback_disabled = bool(args.no_memory_admission_feedback)
  325:     # Full replay is the comprehensive path.  The separate fast stage is the
  326:     # only flat-memory ablation; do not let a forgotten flag silently turn a
  327:     # full SM86 run into the old flat baseline.
  328:     if args.mode in ("both", "full") and args.memory_mode != "l1-l2":
  329:         parser.error("full replay requires --memory-mode l1-l2; flat/l1 are fast-stage-only ablations")
  330:     args.carded_dram_enabled = args.carded_dram if args.carded_dram is not None else args.target_arch == "sm_86"
  331:     args.dram_service_rate_requested = args.dram_service_rate_bytes_per_sec
  332:     args.dram_service_rate_defaulted = False
  333:     # Independent repeated RTX 3090 Ti memtest runs support an effective
  334:     # forced-DRAM warm-rate scenario near 825 GB/s (roughly [810,850] under
  335:     # the unlocked protocol). Promote the absolute-rate midpoint only for the
  336:     # SM86 full path; the simulator maps it onto the locked-card SM clock for
  337:     # cycle pricing. Retain the vendor 1008 GB/s card value as reference and
  338:     # record that the scenario was defaulted rather than supplied by the caller.
  339:     if (
  340:         args.dram_service_rate_bytes_per_sec is None
  341:         and args.target_arch == "sm_86"
  342:         and args.carded_dram_enabled
  343:         and args.mode in ("both", "full")
  344:     ):
  345:         args.dram_service_rate_bytes_per_sec = 825_000_000_000
  346:         args.dram_service_rate_defaulted = True
  347:     if args.no_memory_admission_feedback and args.memory_admission_feedback is not None:
  348:         parser.error("--no-memory-admission-feedback cannot be combined with --memory-admission-feedback")
  349:     if args.memory_admission_feedback is not None and args.cache_scope == "stream-l2":
  350:         parser.error(
  351:             "--memory-admission-feedback currently requires --cache-scope launch; "
  352:             "stream-l2 has no launch-scoped store/fence feedback arm"
  353:         )
  354:     if (
  355:         args.memory_admission_feedback is None
  356:         and not args.no_memory_admission_feedback
  357:         and args.target_arch == "sm_86"
  358:         and args.mode in ("both", "full")
  359:     ):
  360:         if args.cache_scope == "launch":
  361:             args.memory_admission_feedback = DEFAULT_SM86_MEMORY_ADMISSION_FEEDBACK
  362:             args.memory_admission_feedback_defaulted = True
  363:         else:
  364:             # The direct runner deliberately rejects this timing-active arm
  365:             # for persistent stream-L2 scope.  Keep stream-L2 usable as an
  366:             # explicit cache-lifetime ablation and record the scoped gap
  367:             # instead of emitting replay_error rows for every launch.
  368:             args.memory_admission_feedback_scope_suppressed = True
  369:     if args.memory_admission_feedback is not None and not args.memory_admission_feedback.is_file():
  370:         parser.error(f"memory-admission feedback does not exist: {args.memory_admission_feedback}")
  371:     if not 0 < args.timeout < float("inf"):
  372:         parser.error("--timeout must be positive and finite")
  373:     if args.max_sim_output_bytes <= 0:
  374:         parser.error("--max-sim-output-bytes must be positive")
  375:     if args.workers != 12:
  376:         parser.error("--workers must be exactly 12 for every corpus replay")
  377:     if args.sm120_effective_l2 and args.mode == "fast":
  378:         parser.error("--sm120-effective-l2 applies only to the full l1-l2 stage")
  379:     if args.sm120_effective_l2 and args.memory_mode != "l1-l2":
  380:         parser.error("--sm120-effective-l2 requires --memory-mode l1-l2")
  381:     if args.target_arch != "sm_120" and (args.sm120_effective_l1 or args.sm120_effective_l2):
  382:         parser.error("--sm120-effective-l1/--sm120-effective-l2 require an sm_120 architecture card")
  383:     if args.cache_scope == "stream-l2" and args.mode == "fast":
  384:         parser.error("--cache-scope stream-l2 applies only to the full replay stage")
  385:     if args.carded_dram is True and args.mode == "fast":
  386:         parser.error("--carded-dram applies only to the full l1-l2 stage")
  387:     if args.carded_dram_enabled and args.memory_mode != "l1-l2":
  388:         parser.error("--carded-dram requires --memory-mode l1-l2")
  389:     if args.dram_service_rate_bytes_per_sec is not None:
  390:         if args.dram_service_rate_bytes_per_sec <= 0:

  445:                "dram_service_rate_requested": args.dram_service_rate_requested,

  455:     for mode in modes:
  456:         started = time.perf_counter_ns()
  457:         counts = Counter()

  459:         with open_jsonl_writer(args.output / f"{mode}.jsonl") as output:
  460:             def run_one(item):
  461:                 capture, relative, control, error = item
  462:                 return ({"canonical_capture": relative, "capture_dir": str(capture),
  463:                          "status": "control_error", "detail": error} if error else
  464:                         replay(capture, relative, control, args, mode,
  465:                                args.output / f"{mode}_logs"))
  466: 
  467:             with ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=args.workers,
  468:                                     thread_name_prefix=f"replay-{mode}") as pool:
  469:                 # Keep only twelve futures/results live.  ``pool.map`` retains
  470:                 # completed out-of-order rows behind the first slow capture;
  471:                 # that defeated the stdout bound for large JSON documents.
  472:                 pending = {}
  473:                 prepared_iter = iter(prepared)
  474:                 for _ in range(args.workers):
  475:                     try:
  476:                         item = next(prepared_iter)
  477:                     except StopIteration:
  478:                         break
  479:                     pending[pool.submit(run_one, item)] = item
  480:                 index = 0
  481:                 while pending:
  482:                     done, _ = wait(pending, return_when=FIRST_COMPLETED)
  483:                     for future in done:
  484:                         item_for_future = pending.pop(future, (None, "", None, None))
  485:                         index += 1
  486:                         try:
  487:                             row = future.result()
  488:                         except Exception as failure:
  489:                             capture, relative, _control, _error = item_for_future
  490:                             row = {"canonical_capture": relative,
  491:                                    "capture_dir": str(capture) if capture else "",
  492:                                    "status": "runner_error", "detail": str(failure)}
  493:                         try:
  494:                             item = next(prepared_iter)
  495:                         except StopIteration:
  496:                             item = None
  497:                         if item is not None:
  498:                             pending[pool.submit(run_one, item)] = item
  499:                         output.write(json.dumps(row, separators=(",", ":"), allow_nan=False) + "\n")
  500:                         output.flush()
  501:                         counts[row["status"]] += 1
  502:                         if row.get("cache_scope_fallback"):
  503:                             summary["cache_scope_fallbacks"] += 1
  504:                         if mode == "full" and row["status"] == "ok":
  505:                             # Report the actual binary-resolved defaults, not

===== BEGIN tools/sm120_metric_report.py: scope diagnostics =====
  210: 
  211: 
  212: def scope_warnings(launch: dict[str, Any], target_metrics: dict[str, Any]) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
  213:     """Diagnose gross opcode-scope disagreements without changing scoring.
  214: 
  215:     Some captured NCU rows expose only a narrow SASS/pipe scope while the
  216:     trace-side logical memory ledger counts every admitted record.  A large
  217:     disagreement is retained as a post-replay warning, never used to select a
  218:     prediction or tune replay.
  219:     """
  220:     trace = launch.get("trace_metrics", {})
  221:     memory = trace.get("memory", {}) if isinstance(trace, dict) else {}
  222:     warnings: list[dict[str, Any]] = []
  223:     for kind, ncu_metric in (
  224:         ("global_load", "smsp__sass_inst_executed_op_global_ld.sum"),
  225:         ("global_store", "smsp__sass_inst_executed_op_global_st.sum"),
  226:         ("local_load", "smsp__sass_inst_executed_op_local_ld.sum"),
  227:         ("local_store", "smsp__sass_inst_executed_op_local_st.sum"),
  228:     ):
  229:         trace_row = memory.get(kind)
  230:         target_row = target_metrics.get(ncu_metric)
  231:         if not isinstance(trace_row, dict) or not isinstance(target_row, dict):
  232:             continue
  233:         trace_count = finite(trace_row.get("requests"))
  234:         target_count = finite(target_row.get("value"))
  235:         if trace_count is None or target_count is None or trace_count <= 0:
  236:             continue
  237:         ratio = None if target_count <= 0 else max(trace_count / target_count, target_count / trace_count)
  238:         if ratio is not None and ratio < 4.0:
  239:             continue
  240:         warnings.append({
  241:             "kind": "opcode_scope_mismatch",
  242:             "trace_kind": kind,
  243:             "trace_requests": trace_count,
  244:             "ncu_metric": ncu_metric,
  245:             "ncu_value": target_count,
  246:             "ratio": ratio,
  247:             "detail": "post-replay scope diagnostic only; NCU target was not used as a replay input or prediction selector",
  248:         })
  249:     counters = launch.get("counters", {})
  250:     l2_by_source = counters.get("l2_by_source", {}) if isinstance(counters, dict) else {}
  251:     l2_count = sum(
  252:         float(value.get("sectors", 0))
  253:         for value in l2_by_source.values()
  254:         if isinstance(value, dict)
  255:     )
  256:     dram = counters.get("dram", {}) if isinstance(counters, dict) else {}
  257:     dram_count = finite(dram.get("read_sectors")) if isinstance(dram, dict) else None
  258:     for kind, trace_count, ncu_metric in (
  259:         ("l2_source_sectors", l2_count, "lts__t_sectors_srcunit_tex.sum"),
  260:         ("dram_read_sectors", dram_count, "dram__sectors_read.sum"),
  261:     ):
  262:         target_row = target_metrics.get(ncu_metric)
  263:         if not isinstance(target_row, dict) or trace_count is None or trace_count <= 0:
  264:             continue
  265:         target_count = finite(target_row.get("value"))
  266:         if target_count is None:
  267:             continue
  268:         ratio = None if target_count <= 0 else max(trace_count / target_count, target_count / trace_count)
  269:         if ratio is not None and ratio < 4.0:
  270:             continue
  271:         warnings.append({
  272:             "kind": "operation_scope_mismatch",
  273:             "trace_kind": kind,
  274:             "trace_requests": trace_count,
  275:             "ncu_metric": ncu_metric,
  276:             "ncu_value": target_count,
  277:             "ratio": ratio,
  278:             "detail": "post-replay scope diagnostic only; simulator counters and NCU operation domain may not be like-for-like",
  279:         })
  280:     return warnings
  281: 
  282: 
  283: def scope_warning_metrics(warnings: list[dict[str, Any]]) -> set[str]:
  284:     """Expand a scope warning to the metric endpoints it invalidates."""
  285:     metrics: set[str] = set()
  286:     for warning in warnings:
  287:         kind = warning.get("trace_kind")
  288:         if kind == "global_load":
  289:             metrics.update({
  290:                 "smsp__sass_inst_executed_op_global_ld.sum",
  291:                 "l1tex__t_sectors_pipe_lsu_mem_global_op_ld.sum",
  292:                 "l1tex__t_sectors_pipe_lsu_mem_global_op_ld_lookup_hit.sum",
  293:                 "l1tex__t_sectors_pipe_lsu_mem_global_op_ld_lookup_miss.sum",
  294:             })
  295:         elif kind == "global_store":
  296:             metrics.update({
  297:                 "smsp__sass_inst_executed_op_global_st.sum",
  298:                 "l1tex__t_sectors_pipe_lsu_mem_global_op_st.sum",
  299:             })
  300:         elif kind == "local_load":
  301:             metrics.update({
  302:                 "smsp__sass_inst_executed_op_local_ld.sum",
  303:                 "l1tex__t_sectors_pipe_lsu_mem_local_op_ld.sum",
  304:             })
  305:         elif kind == "local_store":
  306:             metrics.update({
  307:                 "smsp__sass_inst_executed_op_local_st.sum",
  308:                 "l1tex__t_sectors_pipe_lsu_mem_local_op_st.sum",
  309:             })
  310:         elif kind == "l2_source_sectors":
  311:             metrics.update({
  312:                 "lts__t_sectors_srcunit_tex.sum",
  313:                 "lts__t_sectors_srcunit_tex_lookup_hit.sum",
  314:                 "lts__t_sectors_srcunit_tex_lookup_miss.sum",
  315:                 "lts__t_sectors_srcunit_tex_op_read.sum",
  316:                 "lts__t_sectors_srcunit_tex_op_read_lookup_hit.sum",
  317:                 "lts__t_sectors_srcunit_tex_op_read_lookup_miss.sum",
  318:             })
  319:         elif kind == "dram_read_sectors":
  320:             metrics.add("dram__sectors_read.sum")
  321:     return metrics
